<h1 class="firstHeading" id="firstHeading" lang="en">Guide to the Systems Engineering Body of Knowledge (SEBoK)</h1>
<div class="mw-body-content" id="bodyContent">
<div id="siteSub">From SEBoK</div>
<div id="contentSub"></div>
<div class="mw-jump" id="jump-to-nav">
					Jump to:					<a href="#mw-head">navigation</a>, 					<a href="#p-search">search</a>
</div>
<div class="mw-content-ltr" dir="ltr" id="mw-content-text" lang="en"><div id="breadcrumbs"> <a href="/wiki/Guide_to_the_Systems_Engineering_Body_of_Knowledge_(SEBoK)" title="Guide to the Systems Engineering Body of Knowledge (SEBoK)">Guide to the Systems Engineering Body of Knowledge (SEBoK)</a></div><div class="mw-parser-output"><p>The SEBoK provides a compendium of the <a class="mw-redirect" href="/wiki/Primary_References" title="Primary References">key knowledge sources and references</a> of <span class="noprint"><a class="cat-glossary_of_terms" href="/wiki/Systems_Engineering_(glossary)" title="Systems Engineering (glossary)"><span class="simple-tooltip simple-tooltip-inline tooltipstered" data-simple-tooltip="&lt;p&gt;The definition of systems engineering has evolved over time. The current accepted definitions are found below:&amp;lt;blockquote&amp;gt;&lt;i&gt;(1) Interdisciplinary approach governing the total technical and managerial effort required to transform a set of customer needs, expectations, and constraints into a solution and to support that solution throughout its life.&lt;/i&gt; (ISO/IEC/IEEE 2010) &amp;lt;/blockquote&amp;gt;&amp;lt;blockquote&amp;gt;&lt;i&gt;(2) An interdisciplinary approach and means to enable the realization of successful systems. It focuses on defining customer needs and required functionality early in the development cycle, documenting requirements, then proceeding with design synthesis and system validation while considering the complete problem: &lt;/i&gt;
&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;i&gt;Operations&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;i&gt;Performance&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;i&gt;Test&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;i&gt;Manufacturing&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;i&gt;Cost &amp;amp; Schedule&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;i&gt;Training &amp;amp; Support&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;li&gt;&lt;i&gt;Disposal&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;i&gt;Systems engineering integrates all the disciplines and specialty groups into a team effort forming a structured development process that proceeds from concept to production to operation. Systems engineering considers both the business and the technical needs of all customers with the goal of providing a quality product that meets the user needs.&lt;/i&gt;  (INCOSE 2012)
&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;i&gt;(3) A transdisiplinary and integrative approach to enable the successful realization, use, and retirement of engineered systems, using systems principles and concepts, and scientific, technological, and management methods.&lt;/i&gt;
&amp;lt;/blockquote&amp;gt;The Fellows Initiative on System and Systems Engineering Definitions was established in 2016, to review current INCOSE definitions of SYSTEM and SYSTEMS ENGINEERING and to recommend any changes necessary to align the definitions to a) current practice, and b) the aspirations of INCOSE’s 2025 Vision. At the January 2019 INCOSE Board of Directors meeting, a new INCOSE definition for &amp;quot;Systems Engineering” was approved. That definition is:&amp;lt;blockquote&amp;gt;&lt;i&gt;(3) A transdisiplinary and integrative approach to enable the successful realization, use, and retirement of engineered systems, using systems principles and concepts, and scientific, technological, and management methods.&lt;/i&gt;&amp;lt;/blockquote&amp;gt;The INCOSE Fellows elaborated this definition by stating:
&lt;/p&gt;">systems engineering</span></a></span><span class="printonly">systems engineering</span> organized and explained to assist a wide variety of <a class="cat-part_1 cat-sebok_users_and_uses" href="/wiki/SEBoK_Users_and_Uses" title="SEBoK Users and Uses">users</a>. It is a living product, accepting community input continuously, with regular refreshes and updates.
</p><p>Systems engineering is an interdisciplinary approach and means to enable the full life cycle of successful <span class="noprint"><a class="cat-glossary_of_terms" href="/wiki/Product_System_(glossary)" title="Product System (glossary)"><span class="simple-tooltip simple-tooltip-inline tooltipstered" data-simple-tooltip="&lt;p&gt;&amp;lt;blockquote&amp;gt; (1)&lt;i&gt; A &amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;noprint&amp;quot;&amp;gt;&lt;a href='/wiki/System_Context_(glossary)' class='cat-glossary_of_terms' title='System Context (glossary)'&gt;'&amp;quot;`UNIQ--item-2--QINU`&amp;quot;'&lt;/a&gt;&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;&amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;printonly&amp;quot;&amp;gt;system context&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt; for which the &amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;noprint&amp;quot;&amp;gt;&lt;a href='/wiki/System-of-Interest_(glossary)' class='cat-glossary_of_terms' title='System-of-Interest (glossary)'&gt;'&amp;quot;`UNIQ--item-6--QINU`&amp;quot;'&lt;/a&gt;&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;&amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;printonly&amp;quot;&amp;gt;system-of-interest&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt; (SoI) is a &amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;noprint&amp;quot;&amp;gt;&lt;a href='/wiki/Product_(glossary)' class='cat-glossary_of_terms' title='Product (glossary)'&gt;'&amp;quot;`UNIQ--item-12--QINU`&amp;quot;'&lt;/a&gt;&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;&amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;printonly&amp;quot;&amp;gt;product&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;.&lt;/i&gt; (Created for SEBoK)&amp;lt;/blockquote&amp;gt;
&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&amp;lt;blockquote&amp;gt;(2) &lt;i&gt;A system considered from the point of view of a physical “system end product” (ANSI/EIA 2003) made of system elements that may include hardware, software, infrastructure and support &amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;noprint&amp;quot;&amp;gt;&lt;a href='/wiki/Service_(glossary)' class='cat-glossary_of_terms' title='Service (glossary)'&gt;'&amp;quot;`UNIQ--item-13--QINU`&amp;quot;'&lt;/a&gt;&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;&amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;printonly&amp;quot;&amp;gt;services&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;. The people and &amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;noprint&amp;quot;&amp;gt;&lt;a href='/wiki/Organizational_(glossary)' class='cat-glossary_of_terms' title='Organizational (glossary)'&gt;'&amp;quot;`UNIQ--item-14--QINU`&amp;quot;'&lt;/a&gt;&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;&amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;printonly&amp;quot;&amp;gt;organizational&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt; aspects of the “whole system” of which the “product system” forms a part have to be considered in the &amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;noprint&amp;quot;&amp;gt;&lt;a href='/wiki/Design_(glossary)' class='cat-glossary_of_terms' title='Design (glossary)'&gt;'&amp;quot;`UNIQ--item-15--QINU`&amp;quot;'&lt;/a&gt;&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;&amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;printonly&amp;quot;&amp;gt;design&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;, but are provided by another &amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;noprint&amp;quot;&amp;gt;&lt;a href='/wiki/Organization_(glossary)' class='cat-glossary_of_terms' title='Organization (glossary)'&gt;'&amp;quot;`UNIQ--item-16--QINU`&amp;quot;'&lt;/a&gt;&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;&amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;printonly&amp;quot;&amp;gt;organization&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;. &lt;/i&gt;(ISO/IEC/IEEE 15288 2008)&amp;lt;/blockquote&amp;gt;
&lt;/p&gt;">product</span></a></span><span class="printonly">product</span>, <span class="noprint"><a class="cat-glossary_of_terms" href="/wiki/Service_System_(glossary)" title="Service System (glossary)"><span class="simple-tooltip simple-tooltip-inline tooltipstered" data-simple-tooltip="&lt;p&gt;&amp;lt;blockquote&amp;gt;&lt;i&gt;A dynamic configuration of resources (people, technology, organizations and shared information) that creates and delivers value between the provider and the customer through services.&lt;/i&gt; (IfM and IBM 2008)&amp;lt;/blockquote&amp;gt;
&lt;/p&gt;">service</span></a></span><span class="printonly">service</span> and <span class="noprint"><a class="cat-glossary_of_terms" href="/wiki/Enterprise_System_(glossary)" title="Enterprise System (glossary)"><span class="simple-tooltip simple-tooltip-inline tooltipstered" data-simple-tooltip="&lt;p&gt;&amp;lt;blockquote&amp;gt;&lt;i&gt;A &amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;noprint&amp;quot;&amp;gt;&lt;a href='/wiki/System_Context_(glossary)' class='cat-glossary_of_terms' title='System Context (glossary)'&gt;'&amp;quot;`UNIQ--item-19--QINU`&amp;quot;'&lt;/a&gt;&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;&amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;printonly&amp;quot;&amp;gt;system context&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt; for which the system-of-interest is an &amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;noprint&amp;quot;&amp;gt;&lt;a href='/wiki/Enterprise_(glossary)' class='cat-glossary_of_terms' title='Enterprise (glossary)'&gt;'&amp;quot;`UNIQ--item-38--QINU`&amp;quot;'&lt;/a&gt;&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;&amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;printonly&amp;quot;&amp;gt;enterprise&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;.&lt;/i&gt; (Created for SEBoK)&amp;lt;/blockquote&amp;gt;
&lt;/p&gt;">enterprise</span></a></span><span class="printonly">enterprise</span> systems.  It includes problem discovery and formulation, solution definition and realization, and operational use, sustainment, and disposal. It can be applied to single problem situations or to the management of multiple interventions in commercial or public enterprises.  Those new to systems engineering can find introductory articles which provide an <a class="cat-introduction_to_systems_engineering cat-part_1" href="/wiki/Systems_Engineering_Overview" title="Systems Engineering Overview">overview of systems engineering</a>, place it in <a class="cat-introduction_to_systems_engineering cat-part_1 cat-topic" href="/wiki/Systems_Engineering:_Historic_and_Future_Challenges" title="Systems Engineering: Historic and Future Challenges">historical context</a>, and discuss its <a class="cat-introduction_to_systems_engineering cat-part_1 cat-topic" href="/wiki/Economic_Value_of_Systems_Engineering" title="Economic Value of Systems Engineering">economic value</a> in <a class="cat-part cat-part_1" href="/wiki/SEBoK_Introduction" title="SEBoK Introduction">Part 1</a> of this body of knowledge.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Welcome_to_SEBoK_v._2.0">Welcome to SEBoK v. 2.0</span></h2>
<p>On behalf of the <a href="/wiki/BKCASE_Governance_and_Editorial_Board" title="BKCASE Governance and Editorial Board">BKCASE Editorial Board</a>, the BKCASE Governing Board, and sponsors, welcome to SEBoK v. 2.0.
</p><p>This version was released on 1 June 2019 and reflects the continuing evolution of the SEBoK. For a summary of the changes made for v. 2.0 see the <a href="/wiki/Letter_from_the_Editor" title="Letter from the Editor">Letter from the Editor</a>. See <a href="/wiki/Acknowledgements_and_Release_History" title="Acknowledgements and Release History">Acknowledgements and Release History</a> for a full description of the current and all previous SEBoK versions.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="About_the_SEBoK">About the SEBoK</span></h2>
<p>Systems engineering has its roots in the fundamentals, principles, and models of foundational systems sciences, and associated management and engineering sciences. It is applied through the application of systems engineering processes within a managed life cycle working with a number of other management, engineering, and specialist disciplines. While traditionally applied to product development, systems engineering can also be applied to <span class="noprint"><a class="cat-glossary_of_terms" href="/wiki/Service_System_(glossary)" title="Service System (glossary)"><span class="simple-tooltip simple-tooltip-inline tooltipstered" data-simple-tooltip="&lt;p&gt;&amp;lt;blockquote&amp;gt;&lt;i&gt;A dynamic configuration of resources (people, technology, organizations and shared information) that creates and delivers value between the provider and the customer through services.&lt;/i&gt; (IfM and IBM 2008)&amp;lt;/blockquote&amp;gt;
&lt;/p&gt;">service</span></a></span><span class="printonly">service</span> and <span class="noprint"><a class="cat-glossary_of_terms" href="/wiki/Enterprise_System_(glossary)" title="Enterprise System (glossary)"><span class="simple-tooltip simple-tooltip-inline tooltipstered" data-simple-tooltip="&lt;p&gt;&amp;lt;blockquote&amp;gt;&lt;i&gt;A &amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;noprint&amp;quot;&amp;gt;&lt;a href='/wiki/System_Context_(glossary)' class='cat-glossary_of_terms' title='System Context (glossary)'&gt;'&amp;quot;`UNIQ--item-19--QINU`&amp;quot;'&lt;/a&gt;&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;&amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;printonly&amp;quot;&amp;gt;system context&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt; for which the system-of-interest is an &amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;noprint&amp;quot;&amp;gt;&lt;a href='/wiki/Enterprise_(glossary)' class='cat-glossary_of_terms' title='Enterprise (glossary)'&gt;'&amp;quot;`UNIQ--item-38--QINU`&amp;quot;'&lt;/a&gt;&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;&amp;lt;span class=&amp;quot;printonly&amp;quot;&amp;gt;enterprise&amp;lt;/span&amp;gt;.&lt;/i&gt; (Created for SEBoK)&amp;lt;/blockquote&amp;gt;
&lt;/p&gt;">enterprise</span></a></span><span class="printonly">enterprise</span> systems. As systems engineering is a collaborative approach, working with other engineering and management disciplines and specialisms, it relies on enabling competencies and structures at individual, team, and organizational levels.
</p><p>Starting from this basic view of the scope of knowledge relevant to SE, the SEBoK is organized into <a href="/wiki/SEBoK_Table_of_Contents" title="SEBoK Table of Contents">7 parts</a> as shown below:
</p>
<div class="center"><div class="thumb tnone"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:654px;"><a class="image" href="/wiki/File:SEBoK_Navigation_Overview.PNG"><img alt="" class="thumbimage" height="485" src="/w/images/sebokwiki-farm!w/c/c1/SEBoK_Navigation_Overview.PNG" width="652"/></a> <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a class="internal" href="/wiki/File:SEBoK_Navigation_Overview.PNG" title="Enlarge"></a></div><b>Figure 1 Scope of SEBoK Parts and related knowledge </b>(SEBoK Original). See <a class="cat-introduction_to_the_sebok cat-part_1" href="/wiki/Structure_of_the_SEBoK" title="Structure of the SEBoK">Structure of the SEBoK</a> for details.</div></div></div></div>
<ul><li>Part 1 <a class="cat-part cat-part_1" href="/wiki/SEBoK_Introduction" title="SEBoK Introduction">SEBoK Introduction</a></li>
<li>Part 2 <a class="cat-pages_where_expansion_depth_is_exceeded cat-part cat-part_2" href="/wiki/Foundations_of_Systems_Engineering" title="Foundations of Systems Engineering">Foundations of Systems Engineering</a></li>
<li>Part 3 <a class="cat-part cat-part_3" href="/wiki/Systems_Engineering_and_Management" title="Systems Engineering and Management">Systems Engineering and Management</a></li>
<li>Part 4 <a class="cat-part cat-part_4" href="/wiki/Applications_of_Systems_Engineering" title="Applications of Systems Engineering">Applications of Systems Engineering</a></li>
<li>Part 5 <a class="cat-pages_where_expansion_depth_is_exceeded cat-part cat-part_5" href="/wiki/Enabling_Systems_Engineering" title="Enabling Systems Engineering">Enabling Systems Engineering</a></li>
<li>Part 6 <a class="cat-part cat-part_6" href="/wiki/Related_Disciplines" title="Related Disciplines">Related Disciplines</a></li>
<li>Part 7 <a class="cat-part cat-part_7" href="/wiki/Systems_Engineering_Implementation_Examples" title="Systems Engineering Implementation Examples">Systems Engineering Implementation Examples</a></li></ul>
<p>The SEBoK also includes a <a class="mw-redirect" href="/wiki/Glossary_of_Terms" title="Glossary of Terms">Glossary of Terms</a> and a list of <a class="mw-redirect" href="/wiki/Primary_References" title="Primary References">Primary References</a>, to reflect this scope of systems engineering knowledge and its links into other bodies of knowledge. 
</p><p>SEBoK is a guide to the broad scope of SE related knowledge.  The core of this is the well tried and test knowledge which has been developed through practice, documented, reviewed and discussed by the SE community.  In addition, SEBoK also covers some of the emerging aspects of SE practice, such as Systems of Systems, Agile Life Cycle approaches or Model Based Systems Engineering (MBSE). Part 1 also includes a discussion of <a class="cat-part_1 cat-sebok_users_and_uses" href="/wiki/SEBoK_Users_and_Uses" title="SEBoK Users and Uses">SEBoK Users and Uses</a>, including a number of <a href="/wiki/Category:Use_Case" title="Category:Use Case">use cases</a> which give advice on how different groups of users might navigate and use the SEBoK.  This is a good place to start if you are new to the SEBoK. Individuals who are new to systems engineering can start with <a class="cat-part cat-part_1" href="/wiki/Use_Case_0:_Systems_Engineering_Novices" title="Use Case 0: Systems Engineering Novices">Use Case 0: Systems Engineering Novices</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="How_to_use_the_SEBoK_Wiki">How to use the SEBoK Wiki</span></h2>
<p>Articles in the SEBoK can be found by using the <i>Search</i> field in the upper right corner of each page, as well as through the <i>Quicklinks</i>, <i>Outline</i>, and <i>Navigation</i> menus in the left margin of each page. Detailed instructions about the page layout and features are found in <a href="/wiki/How_to_Read_the_SEBoK" title="How to Read the SEBoK">How to Read the SEBoK</a>. There is a link in the left margin under <i>Quicklinks</i> explaining how to <a href="/wiki/Cite_the_SEBoK" title="Cite the SEBoK">Cite the SEBoK</a> correctly.
</p><p>As a living document, at the bottom of each page, version identification can be found in a link called "<a href="/wiki/About_the_SEBoK" title="About the SEBoK">About the SEBoK</a>."  A PDF of the SEBoK v. 1.8, as well as archive copies of earlier versions, may be downloaded at <a href="/wiki/Download_SEBoK_PDF" title="Download SEBoK PDF">Download SEBoK PDF</a>.
</p><p><b>Comments can be left on any page of the SEBoK version using the comment feature at the bottom.</b> These are periodically reviewed. Comments can be flagged in DISQUS, which will result in a faster review by the editors. You may also view the current <a href="/wiki/BKCASE_Governance_and_Editorial_Board" title="BKCASE Governance and Editorial Board">Editorial Board</a> and contact editors directly about the materials in their areas of responsibility. All review comments and other updates are managed under an update process, discussed in the next section.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Bkcase_Wiki:Copyright" title="Bkcase Wiki:Copyright">copyright information</a> is placed on each page, and must be respected. The SEBoK copyright is  held on behalf of the BKCASE Board of Governors by The Trustees of the Stevens Institute of Technology. Images and tables marked as "SEBoK Original" may be used with proper citation under creative commons. Images and tables marked as "Used with Permission" are not the property of the SEBoK; individuals using these materials must seek their own permission to reuse.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="SEBoK_Updates_and_Governance">SEBoK Updates and Governance</span></h2>
<p>The <i>Guide to the Systems Engineering Body of Knowledge (SEBoK)</i> was created by the <b>Body of Knowledge and Curriculum to Advance Systems Engineering (<a class="external text" href="http://www.bkcase.org" rel="nofollow">BKCASE</a>)</b> project. BKCASE is overseen by a <a href="/wiki/BKCASE_Governance_and_Editorial_Board#BKCASE_Governing_Board" title="BKCASE Governance and Editorial Board">Governing Board</a>, consisting of the International Council on Systems Engineering (INCOSE), the Systems Engineering Research Center (SERC), and the IEEE Computer Society. 
</p><p>The SEBoK is sometimes compared to Wikipedia. The SEBoK is like Wikipedia in its most fundamental structure, as it is a collection of electronic articles built on MediaWiki technology. However, the SEBoK is unlike Wikipedia in that its content is carefully controlled. Anyone in the community can suggest changes be made to SEBoK articles, but the <a href="/wiki/BKCASE_Governance_and_Editorial_Board#BKCASE_Editorial_Board" title="BKCASE Governance and Editorial Board">Editorial Board</a> will review all recommendations before they are implemented in the SEBoK. Wikipedia is a much more open wiki, allowing virtually anyone to change any article, while reserving the right to undo changes that are offensive or otherwise violate Wikipedia's rules. Tight control over SEBoK content is a trade-off.  Such control ensures a stable baseline whose quality and integrity are assured by its editors.  On the other hand, such control discourages some members of the community from contributing improvements to the SEBoK.
</p><p>New releases of the SEBoK are under the control of the <a href="/wiki/BKCASE_Governance_and_Editorial_Board#BKCASE_Governing_Board" title="BKCASE Governance and Editorial Board">Governing Board</a> appointed by the stewards, who oversee the SEBoK Editor in Chief and Editorial Board. The stewards contribute resources to manage the SEBoK wiki, support new releases, and encourage SEBoK adoption. Volunteer authors from the worldwide SE community continue to propose and create new content and other volunteers review that new content. The stewards are INCOSE, the Systems Engineering Research Center, and the IEEE Computer Society.
</p>
